269→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   270→| `obj.clu{p}(c).trialtm`, `.trial`, with `obj.bp.ev.goCue` | `neural` | align (`trialtm − goCue`), bin into 10 ms bins over [-2.5, 2.5] s, ÷dt, causal-Gaussian smooth (`gausswin(15)`, 7 taps zeroed, 'reflect' boundary) → spikes/s, `float32` (n_neurons, 500) | `alignSpikes`, `getSeq`, `mySmooth` | exactly reproduces `obj.trialdat` |\n   271→| `obj.clu{p}(c).quality` | neuron curation | drop `garbage`/`gabrga`/`noisy`/`real?` (case-sensitive, trimmed) | `findClusters` | |\n   272→| mean of binned rate over window & trials | neuron curation | keep > 1 Hz | `removeLowFRClusters`, Methods | |\n   273→| `obj.time` (= bin centres) | `input[0]` `time_from_go_cue` | continuous ramp −2.495 … +2.495 s, `float32` (1, 500) | `getSeq` | only decoder input, per the task spec |\n   274→| `obj.bp.R/L/hit/miss/no` | `output[0]` `lick_direction` | `0=left` if `(L&hit)|(R&miss)`, `1=right` if `(R&hit)|(L&miss)`, `2=none` if `no` | `getPrevChoice` (same formula) | per trial, broadcast over time |\n   275→| `obj.bp.autowater` | `output[1]` `context` | `0=WC` if `autowater`, else `1=DR` | `params.condition` strings, `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` | per trial |\n   276→| `obj.bp.hit/miss/no` | `output[2]` `outcome` | `0=incorrect` (`miss`), `1=correct` (`hit`), `2=ignore` (`no`) | `getOutcome` | per trial |\n   277→| `obj.traj{1}.ts[:, :2, 'tongue']` + `frameTimes` | `output[3]` `tongue_velocity` | interp to `obj.time` using `frameTimes − vidshift − goCue`; speed = ‖(dx/dbin, dy/dbin)‖ per visible segment; class 2 where the tongue is not labelled or the video does not cover the bin; else 0/1 by the **session** median of visible speeds | `findPosition`, `findVelocity`, `findVideoOffset` | time-varying |\n   278→| `obj.traj{2}.ts[:, :2, 'top_paw'/'bottom_paw']` | `output[4]` `paw_velocity` | as above; speed averaged over whichever paw(s) are visible; class 2 only where **neither** paw is visible | same | time-varying |\n   279→| `motionEnergy_*.mat` `me.data{trial}` (or `obj.me`) | `output[5]` `motion_energy` | interp to `obj.time` using `frameTimes − vidshift − goCue`; class 2 where the video does not cover the bin; else 0/1 by the **session** median of covered bins | `loadMotionEnergy` | time-varying |\n   280→| `obj.ex.anm` / filename | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | 14 mice | `load<ANM>_ALMVideo` | |\n   281→| ALM probe from load script | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | all units labelled `ALM` | `load<ANM>_ALMVideo`, paper | |\n   282→\n   283→### Key Decisions\n   284→\n   285→1. **Which sessions.** All 44 sessions that contain ALM electrophysiology *and* video and are\n   286→   listed in the reference `DataLoadingScripts/Recording and video/load*_ALMVideo.m` scripts:\n   287→   25 fixed-delay + 19 randomized-delay. The two behaviour-only optogenetic directories have no\n   288→   spikes and cannot be used by a neural decoder. The 3 `data_structure` files on disk that the\n   289→   load scripts omit are excluded (this reproduces the paper's n = 19 randomized-delay sessions).\n   290→   *Rationale*: the requested outputs (lick direction, outcome, kinematics, motion energy) are\n   291→   defined in every session; `context` is degenerate (all DR) in the 32 sessions without WC\n   292→   blocks but is still a correct label there, and the harness computes **balanced** accuracy\n   293→   pooled over sessions, so keeping them adds data without corrupting the context label. The\n   294→   12 two-context sessions supply the WC class.\n   295→2. **Alignment: go cue** (`obj.bp.ev.goCue`), as required by the task and as used by every\n   296→   reference analysis (`params.alignEvent = 'goCue'`). In WC trials this field holds the\n   297→   water-drop time, which is the matched event for that context.\n   298→3. **Window and binning: [-2.5, +2.5] s, 10 ms bins (500 bins/trial)** — exactly\n   299→   `params.tmin/tmax/dt` from every reference figure script. Same length for all trials/sessions.\n   300→4. **Neural representation: smoothed firing rate in spikes/s** (`obj.trialdat`), i.e. the same\n   301→   quantity the paper feeds to its own choice/context decoders, including the causal 150 ms\n   302→   Gaussian kernel with `'reflect'` boundary handling.\n   303→5. **Neuron curation**: reference quality filter + >1 Hz mean firing rate (Methods).\n   304→   Brain region: `ALM` for all units (paper; probe selection from the load scripts).\n   305→6. **Trial curation**: drop `early` (Methods: \"omitted from all analyses\") and `stim.enable`\n   306→   (photoinactivation) trials. Keep hit/miss/ignore and DR/WC — required by the output spec.\n   307→7. **Session curation**: ≥10 curated units (Methods) and ≥2 trials (harness requirement).\n   308→8. **Discretisation threshold**: per session, the median (50th percentile) over all *valid*\n   309→   (visible / video-covered) timepoints of all kept trials in that session. Invalid timepoints\n   310→   are class 2 and are excluded from the percentile, otherwise the \"not visible\" bins (≈90% of\n   311→   the tongue trace) would drag the threshold to 0.\n   312→9. **\"Not visible\" / \"no video\"** covers both DLC drop-out and bins outside the video's temporal\n   313→   coverage. The video starts ≈0.03 s into the trial and ends 5–9 s in, so short-delay trials\n   314→   genuinely have no video at the start of the window and some randomized-delay trials have\n   315→   none at the end. Three trials in the whole dataset have unusable `frameTimes` (all NaN);\n   316→   their kinematic/ME outputs are entirely class 2.\n   317→10. **Paw**: mean speed of the visible subset of {`top_paw`, `bottom_paw`} (bottom camera only,\n   318→    as in the Methods). Using a single paw would make \"not visible\" reach 88–96% in some\n   319→    sessions because DLC loses one paw or the other; the union is 0–17%.\n   320→11. **Tongue**: side-camera `tongue` feature (`params.traj_features{1}{1}`), the canonical\n   321→    tongue-tip marker.\n   322→12. **Velocity units** are pixels per 10 ms bin (the reference's `gradient()` on the interpolated\n   323→    position, no unit conversion). Only the within-session rank matters after the median split.\n   324→13. **No neural/video lag** (`params.advance_movement = 0`), per all reference scripts.\n   325→\n   326→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   327→- [ ] Session/mouse counts: 44 sessions, 14 mice; fixed-delay subset 25/10; randomized 19/4;\n   328→      two-context subset 12 sessions.\n   329→- [ ] Unit counts vs. paper: two-context subset ≈522 pre-FR-filter (we get 528).\n   330→- [ ] Every session ≥10 units; report the distribution (paper ED Fig. 2a).\n   331→- [ ] `hit + miss + no == Ntrials` per session; kept trials = Ntrials − |early ∪ stim|.\n   332→- [ ] Input range exactly [-2.495, 2.495]; identical time base for every trial.\n   333→- [ ] Output 0/1/2 fractions per variable; tongue class-2 fraction ≈0.9 (matches raw DLC NaN rate).\n   334→- [ ] Per session, class-0 and class-1 counts for each discretised output are equal ±1\n   335→      (median split by construction).\n   336→- [ ] Spike-count spot check: recompute the binned rate for a specific (session, trial, neuron)\n   337→      straight from the raw `.mat` and compare with `np.allclose`.\n   338→- [ ] Behaviour spot check: recompute lick direction / outcome / context for specific trials\n   339→      from `bp` fields.\n   340→- [ ] Kinematics spot check: recompute the interpolated tongue/paw position and motion energy\n   341→      for a specific trial and verify the class assignment.\n   342→- [ ] Alignment check: mean firing rate and mean motion energy locked to the go cue should show\n   343→      a sharp rise at t = 0 (plots in `--show-processing`).\n   344→\n   345→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   346→\n   347→---\n   348→\n   349→## Step 6: Script Development\n   350→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   351→\n   352→`/app/convert_data.py` implements the plan above. Structure:\n   353→\n   354→| Function | Mirrors |\n   355→|---|---|\n   356→| `load_obj` | `loadObjs.m` (via `pymatreader`, handles MAT v7 and v7.3) |\n   357→| `select_clusters` | `findClusters.m` with `quality={'all'}` |\n   358→| `select_trials` | the `~stim.enable & ~early` part of every `params.condition` |\n   359→| `causal_gaussian` / `my_smooth` | `mySmooth.m` (`gausswin(15)`, first 7 taps zeroed, `'reflect'`) |\n   360→| `bin_index` | `histc(t, tmin:dt:tmax)` then `N(1:end-1)` |\n   361→| `bin_and_smooth` | `alignSpikes.m` + `getSeq.m` → `obj.trialdat` |\n   362→| `video_shift` | `findVideoOffset.m` |\n   363→| `interp_feature` | `findPosition.m` |\n   364→| `feature_speed` | `findVelocity.m` |\n   365→| `load_motion_energy` + `interp_trace` | `loadMotionEnergy.m` |\n   366→| mean-rate filter in `process_session` | `removeLowFRClusters.m` |\n   367→\n   368→CLI: `python -u /app/convert_data.py <out.pkl> [--full|--sample] [--show-processing] [--workers N]`.\n   369→\n   370→**Verification of the two most error-prone primitives** (`/app/cache/test_primitives.py`):\n   371→- `my_smooth` reproduces a literal transcription of `mySmooth.m`\n   372→  (`np.convolve(concat(x[:15], x), kern, 'same')[15:]`) to 2.2e-16, and an impulse at\n   373→  bin 100 produces no output before bin 100 (causality) with its peak exactly at bin 100.\n   374→- `causal_gaussian()` = `[0]*7 + [0.25098, 0.23547, 0.19447, 0.14137, 0.09046, 0.05096,\n   375→  0.02527, 0.01103]`, sums to 1 — matches `gausswin(15)` (alpha = 2.5) with `kern(1:7)=0`.\n   376→- `bin_index` reproduces `histc` edge semantics: `t = -2.5 → bin 0`, `t = 0 → bin 250`\n   377→  (whose centre is +0.005 s), `t = 2.4999 → bin 499`, `t = 2.5 → dropped` (this is the\n   378→  `N(1:end-1)` step), `t` outside `[-2.5, 2.5)` dropped.\n   379→\n   380→Code inefficiencies identified:\n   381→- `pymatreader` reads the whole `obj`, including `clu.spkWavs`, which dominates the runtime\n   382→  (6–8 s and ~1.4 GB for the largest 290 MB session).\n   383→- Naive per-trial × per-neuron Python loops for binning would be ~10^4 iterations/session.\n   384→\n   385→Code speedups added:\n   386→- Spike binning is a single `np.bincount` per cluster over a flattened `(trial, bin)` index\n   387→  instead of a per-trial loop.\n   388→- Smoothing is applied once per session to a `(515, nclusters·ntrials)` matrix, as an 8-tap\n   389→  causal FIR (the kernel's first 7 taps are zero), so no FFT and no round-off.\n   390→- Sessions are processed in parallel with `ProcessPoolExecutor` (12–16 workers; each worker\n   391→  peaks at ~1.5 GB, well within the 1 TB available).\n   392→- Outputs are stored as `int8` and the (identical) time-ramp input array is shared between\n   393→  trials, which keeps the pickle dominated by the neural `float32` arrays.\n   394→\n   395→---\n   396→\n   397→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   398→**Status**: All were inspected and show no anomalies:\n   432→1. **raster → binning → smoothing** for the highest-rate unit of one trial: the smoothed\n   433→   trace rises only *after* each spike (causal kernel) and its peak height matches the\n   434→   binned rate scaled by the kernel peak.\n   435→2. **Go-cue-aligned population PSTH**: a sharp rise exactly at t = 0 and, for the\n   436→   fixed-delay session, a second bump at t ≈ −2.2 s — the sample-tone onset\n   437→   (`ev.sample` = 0.3 s, `ev.goCue` = 2.5 s → −2.2 s). (Sessions differ in\n   469→size; the estimate above uses the largest observed per-session load time, 7.6 s, for the\n   470→25 fixed-delay sessions, which are the big ones.)\n   471→\n   472→### Format verification\n   473→`/app/verification_sample_out.txt`: **\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\"**\n   474→All per-session `T` = 500, input range [-2.5, 2.5], output ranges 0–2 / 0–1 as designed,\n   475→`ALM: 98 neurons`.\n   476→\n   477→---\n   478→\n   479→## Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   480→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   481→\n   482→`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl` → `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   483→\n   484→### Format Validation\n   485→- Errors: None\n   486→- Warnings: None\n   487→\n   488→### Training\n   489→Loss decreased monotonically, 8.10 → 0.734 over 200 epochs; Investigating the raw\n   537→files showed that in those two sessions the **SpikeGLX recording stops before the\n   538→behavioural session does**: the largest trial index appearing in any cluster's spike train\n   539→is 314 of 343, and 312 of 346 respectively. JEB12 2022-05-12 similarly has one trial\n   540→(index 192) with no spikes, which `obj.trials.bp.haveEphys` also flags as 0.\n   541→`trials_with_ephys()` was added to `convert_data.py`: a trial is dropped when **no**\n   542→cluster on the ALM probe(s) — including clusters the quality filter rejects — has a single\n   543→spike on it. That removes 64 trials (29 + 34 + 1) and eliminates all warnings.\n   544→(`obj.trials.bp.haveEphys` was not used directly because its length disagrees with\n   545→`bp.Ntrials` in the one session to 1.3%, and the curation rule is taken\n   579→verbatim from `findClusters.m`. The fixed-delay (−5%) and randomized-delay (+12%)\n   580→discrepancies therefore reflect the paper counting over a slightly different probe/session\n   581→set (e.g. both probes of the dual-probe sessions, or a session that is not in the public\n   582→release), not a different curation rule. The direction of the two errors is opposite, which\n   583→rules out a systematic filtering difference on our side.\n   584→\n   585→### Spot checks of data integrity\n   586→See `/app/cache/sanity_checks.py` (Step 10, Check 2) — all pass.\n   587→\n   588→---\n   589→\n   590→## Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   591→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   592→\n   593→### Check 1 — Output log verification (`/app/verification_full_out.txt`)\n   594→\n   595→**Iteration 1**: 0 errors, **61 warnings**, all of the form\n   596→`Session <s>, trial <t>: all neural data is zero`, concentrated in sessions 36\n   597→(JEB24 2023-10-23, 28 trials) and 43 (JEB24 2023-11-03, 33 trials).\n   598→*Cause*: the ephys recording ends before the behavioural session (last trial with any\n   599→sorted spike = 314/343 and 312/346). *Fix*: `trials_with_ephys()` drops trials with no\n   600→spikes on any cluster of the ALM probe(s) (64 trials in total across the dataset).\n   601→See Step 9.\n   602→\n   603→**Iteration 2 (current)**: `Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.`\n   604→No warnings remain, so nothing had to be left unfixed.\n   605→\n   606→### Check 2 — Independent sanity checks (`/app/cache/sanity_checks.py`)\n   607→\n   608→The script re-derives every quantity from the raw `.mat` files with a literal\n   609→transcription of the MATLAB code (`histc` implemented as an explicit half-open interval\n   610→count, `mySmooth` as `np.convolve(concat(x[:15], x), gausswin_causal, 'same')[15:]`,\n   611→`findVideoOffset` from `mode(sglx.bitcode.bitstart)/fs − mode(bp.ev.bitStart)`) and\n   612→compares with `np.allclose` / `np.array_equal`. It imports **only the session table**\n   613→from `convert_data.py`, never the processing functions. Provenance for the comparison\n   614→comes from the `trial_index` and `cluster_index` arrays now stored in\n   615→`metadata['session_info']`.\n   616→\n   617→Checks run on 4 sessions (`JEB6_2021-04-18`, `JEB13_2022-09-14`, `JEB23_2023-10-11`,\n   618→`JEB24_2023-11-03`), 2 trials and 2 neurons each — **all 44 assertions pass**:\n   619→\n   620→| Check | Example result |\n   621→|---|---|\n   622→| neural firing-rate trace, (session, trial, neuron) | `max|diff| = 3.7e-06` (float32 storage precision), e.g. 108 spikes → peak 98.0 spk/s |\n   623→| lick_direction from `bp.R/L/hit/miss/no` on 3 trials/session | e.g. `R=1,L=0,hit=1 → right`; `R=0,L=1,no=1 → none` |\n   624→| context from `bp.autowater` | exact |\n   625→| outcome from `bp.hit/no` | exact |\n   626→| tongue / paw / motion-energy class vectors for a whole trial | exact (`np.array_equal`) |\n   627→| stored per-session threshold really is the median of valid timepoints | class-1 count ≥ class-0 count, imbalance < 5% |\n   628→| `input` == bin centres, identical for every trial of every session | `[-2.495 .. 2.495]` |\n   629→| trial bookkeeping (`ntrials_kept == len(trial_index)`, unique, in range) | all 44 sessions |\n   630→\n   631→Output: `ALL SANITY CHECKS PASSED`.\n   632→\n   633→Additional primitive tests: `/app/cache/test_primitives.py` (Step 6) — kernel, smoother\n   634→and binning semantics.\n   635→\n   636→### Check 3 — Reference code comparison\n   637→\n   638→| Stage | Reference (MATLAB) | This conversion | Same? |\n   639→|---|---|---|---|\n   640→| **(a) data loading** | `loadObjs.m`: `load(meta(i).datapth)`; session list and ALM probe from `load<ANM>_ALMVideo.m`; motion energy from `motionEnergy_<ANM>_<DATE>.mat` (`loadMotionEnergy.m`) | `load_obj` (pymatreader), identical session/probe table hard-coded from the same scripts, `load_motion_energy` reads the same files (falling back to `obj.me` for the JEB23/JEB24 layout the reference loader does not handle) | ✅ |\n   641→| **(b) neuron filtering** | `findClusters(quality,'all')`: drop `garbage`/`gabrga`/`noisy`/`real?` (case-sensitive, `strtrim`ed); then `removeLowFRClusters` with `params.lowFR = 1` | `select_clusters` — identical string set and case sensitivity; then mean rate over the analysis window > 1 Hz | ✅ |\n   642→| **(b) trial filtering** | every `params.condition` contains `~stim.enable&~early`; conditions also split by `hit/miss/no`, `R/L`, `autowater`, but only to *label* trials | `select_trials` drops `early` and `stim.enable`; hit/miss/no and DR/WC are kept and used as labels, as the decoder-output spec demands | ✅ (plus the no-ephys rule, see below) |\n   643→| **(c) temporal alignment** | `alignSpikes.m`: `trialtm − bp.ev.goCue(trial)`. Video: `frameTimes − findVideoOffset(obj) − ev.goCue(trial)` (`findPosition.m`, `loadMotionEnergy.m`), `params.advance_movement = 0` | identical formulas, same `findVideoOffset` | ✅ |\n   644→| **(d) binning** | `getSeq.m`: `edges = -2.5:0.01:2.5`, `histc`, drop the last bin, `/dt`, `mySmooth(...,15,'reflect')`; `obj.time = edges(1:end-1) + dt/2` | `bin_index` + `np.bincount` (exact `histc` semantics, verified), `/DT`, `my_smooth` (verified equal to `mySmooth` to 2e-16) | ✅ |\n   645→| **(e) input construction** | `obj.time` is the reference time base | `input[0] = obj.time` broadcast over the trial | ✅ (the input set is dictated by the task spec) |\n   646→| **(f) output construction** | `getPrevChoice.m` `choice = (R&hit)|(L&miss)`, NaN on `no`; `getOutcome.m` `outcome = hit`, NaN on `no`; context from `autowater`; kinematics from `findPosition`/`findVelocity`; motion energy from `loadMotionEnergy` | identical definitions, with the reference's `NaN` on ignore trials replaced by the explicit third class the task spec requires (`none` / `ignore`) | ✅ with the spec-mandated change |\n   647→\n   648→**Deliberate differences, and why**\n   649→\n   650→1. **Trials with no electrophysiology are dropped** (64 trials). The reference has no such\n   651→   rule because the MATLAB pipeline simply leaves those trials as all-zero rows in\n   652→   `obj.trialdat` and the figures use trial subsets that happen to avoid them. An all-zero\n   653→   neural matrix is not data and the harness flags it; dropping is required for a decoder.\n   654→2. **`ignore` trials keep a label instead of `NaN`.** `getOutcome.m`/`getPrevChoice.m` set\n   655→   NaN so that MATLAB analyses skip them. The task spec requires `none` and `ignore` as\n   656→   explicit classes, so they are coded 2.\n   657→3. **Velocity is differentiated per contiguous visible segment.** `findVelocity.m` runs\n   658→   `gradient()` over the whole trial and then replaces NaN with 0 (tongue) or the nearest\n   659→   value (other features), which is appropriate for assembling a dense regressor matrix but\n   660→   injects spurious zero velocities at the edge of every visibility gap. Because the task\n   661→   spec makes \"not visible\" its own class, the velocity only has to be defined where the\n   662→   feature *is* visible, and a per-segment derivative is the correct estimate there. The\n   663→   reference's non-tongue baseline-drift subtraction (`median(diff(pos))`) is reproduced,\n   664→   including its use of `basederiv(1)` for both axes (an apparent typo whose numerical\n   665→   effect is negligible — both medians are ≈0 px/bin).\n   666→4. **Paw velocity is the mean over the visible paws** rather than one fixed marker: DLC\n   667→   loses `top_paw` on up to 88% of frames in some sessions and `bottom_paw` on up to 96% in\n   668→   others, so a single marker would make \"not visible\" the dominant class for reasons that\n   669→   have nothing to do with the animal. The union is unavailable on only 0.6–24% of bins.\n   670→5. **No PCA/dimensionality reduction of the kinematic features** (`getKinematics.m` does\n   671→   this for its regression analyses). The decoder task asks for specific discretised\n   672→   kinematic variables, not latent factors.\n   673→6. **Both fixed-delay and randomized-delay sessions are included in one dataset.** The paper\n   674→   analyses them separately; the harness trains a per-session projection, so mixing them is\n   675→   harmless and keeps all available ALM recordings with video.\n   676→\n   677→### Check 4 — Key statistics comparison\n   678→See the table in Step 9. Sessions (44 = 25 + 19), mice per group, two-context unit count\n   679→(529 vs. 522), trials (14,972 raw, 13,762 kept), bin size, window, input range, and all six\n   680→output distributions were compared against the paper, the reference code and the raw data.\n   681→The only residual mismatches are the fixed-delay (−5%) and randomized-delay (+12%) total\n   682→unit counts, investigated in Step 9 and attributed to the paper's counting rather than to a\n   683→curation difference (the discrepancies go in opposite directions, and the group with the\n   684→most explicitly stated composition — the 12 two-context sessions — matches to 1.3%).\n   685→\n   686→Additional behavioural cross-checks computed from the converted data\n   687→(`/app/cache/check_statistics.py`):\n   688→- DR hit rate excluding ignore trials **0.857**, WC **0.915** — consistent with animals\n   689→  being trained to >70% accuracy on the DR task and with WC trials requiring only reward\n   690→  consumption.\n   691→- WC trials are **31.5%** of kept trials in the 12 two-context sessions: the Methods\n   692→  describe ≈100 DR trials followed by alternating 10–25 trial blocks, which for a ~300-trial\n   693→  session predicts ≈33%.\n   694→- Every session has ≥17 units (Methods threshold: ≥10), mean 55.8.\n   695→- Session mean firing rates span 5.4–14.6 spk/s — normal for ALM.\n   696→\n   697→### Check 5 — Edge cases\n   698→\n   699→| Edge case | Finding | Handling |\n   700→|---|---|---|\n   701→| 1-based MATLAB trial indices in `clu.trial` | confirmed 1-based (max == `Ntrials`) | `−1` on load; verified by the spot checks, which map converted trial 5 → raw trial 6 etc. |\n   702→| `histc` last-bin semantics | `histc` returns 501 bins and the code drops the last | `bin_index` drops `t == 2.5` exactly; unit-tested |\n   703→| bin-centre convention | `obj.time = edges + dt/2` then truncated | bin 250 centre = +0.005 s, i.e. the go cue falls at the *start* of bin 250; unit-tested |\n   704→| empty / DUMMY probes | JEB6 probe 1 is an empty array; JEB15 2022-07-29 probe 1 is labelled `DUMMY` | neither is in the reference probe list; `select_clusters` also skips non-dict/missing entries |\n   705→| single-element MATLAB cell arrays | pymatreader unwraps them | `as_cell_list` re-wraps |\n   706→| clusters with a non-string quality (`[]`) | 7 across the dataset | treated as `''`, which is not in the reject list, i.e. kept — exactly what `findClusters.m` does |\n   707→| case-sensitive quality labels | JEB6 has one `Noisy` (capital N) | kept, reproducing MATLAB `ismember(...,'noisy')` |\n   708→| `featNames` differing between trials | checked: identical in all 44 sessions (7 side / 10 bottom features) | `feature_index` still scans until it finds a usable list |\n   709→| trials with unusable `frameTimes` (all-NaN) | exactly 3 in the whole dataset (EKH1 2021-08-07 #178, JEB13 2022-09-24 #186, JEB19 2023-04-19 #222); 2 of the 3 are early-lick/stim trials that are dropped anyway | the remaining one (JEB19 2023-04-19 → converted trial 208) is entirely class 2 for all three video outputs — verified |\n   710→| motion-energy trace length vs `frameTimes` | equal for every trial of every session (checked all 44) | a mismatch would fall back to class 2 |\n   711→| video not covering the whole window | video starts ≈0.03 s into the trial and ends 5–9 s in, so short-delay trials have no video before the window start and some long-delay randomized trials none after +2.5 s | those bins are class 2, which is exactly what \"no video\" means |\n   712→| `bp` field NaNs / ambiguity | none: no NaN in `R/L/hit/miss/no/early/autowater/goCue`; `R` xor `L` for every trial; `hit + miss + no == Ntrials` in all 44 sessions | nothing to handle |\n   713→| ties at the median | `motion_energy` in 5 sessions has up to 1.9% more class-1 than class-0 bins because the raw motion-energy values are quantised and many bins equal the median exactly | correct behaviour of the specified `>=` rule; median \\|n0 − n1\\| over all 132 session × output combinations is **1** |\n   714→| sessions below the harness' 2-trial minimum, or below 10 units | none | rule implemented anyway |\n   715→\n   716→### Iteration log\n   717→1. **Iteration 1** — full conversion → 61 \"all neural data is zero\" warnings →\n   718→   diagnosed as the ephys recording ending early in two JEB24 sessions →\n   719→   added `trials_with_ephys()` → re-ran conversion, verification, statistics and all\n   720→   sanity checks → 0 errors, 0 warnings, all checks pass.\n   721→2. **Iteration 2** — added `trial_index` / `cluster_index` provenance arrays so the\n   722→   sanity checks could address raw trials/clusters directly → re-ran conversion and all\n   723→   checks → unchanged statistics, all checks pass.\n   724→\n   725→---\n   726→\n   727→## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   728→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   729→\n   730→`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples`\n   731→→ `/app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`, `sample_trials.png`, `predictions.png`.\n   732→Trained on GPU, 11,015 training trials / 2,747 validation trials, 200 epochs, ~2.5 min.\n   733→\n   734→### Training Progress\n   735→- Loss decreasing: **Yes**, monotonically — 7.155 → 0.718 over 200 epochs. Test loss 0.762.\n   736→\n   737→### Decoder Results (Full)\n   738→| Output | Chance (1/nclasses) | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Val / chance | Notes |\n   739→|--------|------|-------------|--------|------|-------|\n   740→| lick_direction  | 0.333 | 0.6551 | **0.6431** | 1.93× | 3 classes (left/right/none) over all trials and all timepoints |\n   741→| context         | 0.500 | 0.8649 | **0.8454** | 1.69× | highest of all outputs, as in the paper |\n   742→| outcome         | 0.333 | 0.6379 | **0.6187** | 1.86× | |\n   743→| tongue_velocity | 0.333 | 0.5818 | **0.5755** | 1.73× | 91% of timepoints are \"not visible\" |\n   744→| paw_velocity    | 0.333 | 0.5831 | **0.5741** | 1.72× | |\n   745→| motion_energy   | 0.333 | 0.7224 | **0.7142** | 2.14× | |\n   746→\n   747→Every output is comfortably above chance, and training and validation accuracies differ by\n   748→≤0.02, i.e. the model is if anything under-fitting — there is no data leakage.\n   749→\n   750→---\n   751→\n   752→## Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   753→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   754→\n   755→### Check 1 — Accuracy vs chance\n   756→\n   757→| Output | classes | chance | validation balanced acc | ratio | ≥1.5× chance? |\n   758→|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n   759→| lick_direction | 3 | 0.333 | 0.6431 | 1.93× | ✅ |\n   760→| context | 2 | 0.500 | 0.8454 | 1.69× | ✅ |\n   761→| outcome | 3 | 0.333 | 0.6187 | 1.86× | ✅ |\n   762→| tongue_velocity | 3 | 0.333 | 0.5755 | 1.73× | ✅ |\n   763→| paw_velocity | 3 | 0.333 | 0.5741 | 1.72× | ✅ |\n   764→| motion_energy | 3 | 0.333 | 0.7142 | 2.14× | ✅ |\n   765→\n   766→No output is below chance and none is below 1.5× chance.\n   767→\n   768→**Why the two velocity outputs are the lowest** (`/app/cache/analyze_decoder.py`,\n   769→per-class recall on the validation set):\n   770→\n   771→| Output | class 0 recall | class 1 recall | class 2 recall |\n   772→|---|---|---|---|\n   773→| tongue_velocity | 0.440 (below median) | 0.566 (above median) | 0.710 (not visible) |\n   774→| paw_velocity | 0.451 | 0.482 | 0.790 (not visible) |\n   775→| motion_energy | 0.597 | 0.725 | 0.815 (no video) |\n   776→| lick_direction | 0.561 (left) | 0.590 (right) | 0.732 (none) |\n   777→| context | 0.907 (WC) | 0.824 (DR) | — |\n   778→| outcome | 0.532 | 0.572 | 0.733 |\n   779→\n   780→The \"is the feature visible at all\" distinction is decoded well (0.71–0.82), while\n   781→\"fast vs slow *given that it is visible*\" is the hard part. That is expected rather than a\n   782→bug: within a single lick (~100 ms) the tongue speed alternates between near-zero at the\n   783→protrusion peak and high during protrusion/retraction on a 10–20 ms timescale, whereas the\n   784→neural regressor is a firing rate smoothed with the reference 150 ms **causal** Gaussian\n   785→kernel, which cannot resolve those alternations. The same argument applies to the paws.\n   786→Motion energy, which is a smooth envelope rather than a signed derivative, reaches 0.71.\n   787→\n   788→**Time-resolved validation accuracy** (`/app/cache/decoder_accuracy_vs_time.png`) confirms\n   789→that every output's accuracy has the causally correct time course:\n   790→\n   791→| Output | ITI / pre-sample (t < −2 s) | late delay (−0.5…0 s) | response (0…1 s) | peak |\n   792→|---|---|---|---|---|\n   793→| lick_direction | 0.512 | 0.663 | 0.708 | 0.745 at +0.20 s |\n   794→| context | 0.853 | 0.873 | 0.869 | 0.892 at −0.26 s |\n   795→| outcome | 0.552 | 0.587 | 0.669 | 0.730 at +0.62 s |\n   796→| motion_energy | 0.636 | 0.670 | 0.708 | 0.745 at +0.99 s |\n   797→\n   798→Lick direction is essentially at chance before the sample tone and rises monotonically\n   799→through the delay, peaking just after the go cue — the signature of the paper's Fig. 3b.\n   800→Outcome only becomes decodable *after* the go cue, when reward delivery/omission happens —\n   801→the decoder is not seeing the future. Context is high and flat across all epochs including\n   802→the ITI, which is precisely the paper's central claim (Fig. 4b).\n   803→\n   804→### Check 2 — Accuracy comparison to the paper\n   805→\n   806→The paper reports these decoding numbers:\n   807→\n   808→| Paper analysis | Paper value | This dataset | Comment |\n   809→|---|---|---|---|\n   810→| Choice from delay-epoch CD_choice, ROC (ED Fig. 2c) | AUC 0.86 ± 0.11 | — | CD-projection analysis, not reproduced here |\n   811→| **Choice (L vs R) from neural population, per time bin (Fig. 3b)** | ≈0.5 before the sample tone, rising through the delay to **≈0.9** just after the go cue | **0.525 pre-sample, 0.742 delay, 0.857 response, peak 0.894 at +0.25 s** | ✅ reproduced |\n   812→| **Context (WC vs DR) from neural population, per time bin (Fig. 4b)** | clearly above chance during the ITI (≈0.7) rising to **≈0.9** around the response epoch | **0.739 pre-sample/ITI, 0.751 delay, 0.797 response, peak 0.914 at +0.10 s** | ✅ reproduced |\n   813→| CD_context predicted from video (Fig. 4d) | R² = 0.49 ± 0.22 | — | regression analysis, not reproduced here |\n   814→\n   815→Those numbers come from `/app/cache/paper_style_decoder.py`, which runs **the paper's own\n   816→analysis** on the converted data: per-session, per-time-bin, ridge-regularised logistic\n   817→regression on the raw firing rates of the simultaneously recorded units, equal numbers of\n   818→correct left and right trials, 4 repeats with 30% of trials held out — exactly the recipe\n   819→in the Methods section \"Choice and context decoding from neural population or kinematic\n   820→features\". The resulting curves\n   821→(`/app/cache/paper_style_decoding.png`) reproduce Fig. 3b and Fig. 4b closely, including\n   822→the step at t ≈ −2.2 s where the sample tone occurs.\n   823→\n   824→**Therefore the gap between the paper's ≈0.9 and the harness' 0.64/0.85 is a property of\n   825→the decoder, not of the converted data.** The two differ in three ways that each cost\n   826→accuracy: (i) the harness trains **one shared linear readout across all 44 sessions and all\n   827→500 timepoints**, whereas the paper fits a **separate model for every session and every\n   828→time bin**; (ii) the harness sees 100 shared PCs per session instead of the full population;\n   829→(iii) the harness scores **all** trials (including ignore and error trials) and all\n   830→timepoints, including the 2.5 s before the go cue where choice is genuinely undecodable,\n   831→whereas the paper scores only balanced correct trials at one time bin at a time.\n   832→Restricting the harness' own predictions to the paper's conditions (correct trials only for\n   833→choice, two-context sessions only for context) already closes part of the gap:\n   834→choice 0.768 in the response epoch (peak 0.814) and context 0.736 in the late delay\n   835→(peak 0.767).\n   836→\n   837→### Check 3 — Train vs validation gap\n   838→\n   839→| Output | train | validation | ratio |\n   840→|---|---|---|---|\n   841→| lick_direction | 0.6551 | 0.6431 | 1.019 |\n   842→| context | 0.8649 | 0.8454 | 1.023 |\n   843→| outcome | 0.6379 | 0.6187 | 1.031 |\n   844→| tongue_velocity | 0.5818 | 0.5755 | 1.011 |\n   845→| paw_velocity | 0.5831 | 0.5741 | 1.016 |\n   846→| motion_energy | 0.7224 | 0.7142 | 1.011 |\n   847→\n   848→All ratios are ≤ 1.03, far below the 1.5 threshold: no overfitting and no data leakage.\n   849→(Leakage is also structurally impossible for the per-trial outputs: the split is by trial,\n   850→and a trial's label is constant within the trial.)\n   851→\n   852→### Debugging steps applied to the lower-accuracy outputs\n   853→1. **Output values verified against the raw data on specific trials** — Step 10, Check 2,\n   854→   `np.array_equal` on the whole class vector for tongue, paw and motion energy in 4\n   855→   sessions, and on lick/context/outcome for 12 trials.\n   856→2. **Temporal alignment verified by plotting neural + output for single trials**\n   857→   (`processing_*.png` panels 1, 2, 4, 5, 8) and by the population PSTH, which peaks at\n   858→   t = 0 and shows the sample-tone response at exactly −2.2 s.\n   859→3. **Output variation checked** — no output is dominated by one class in a way that was not\n   860→   intended: the only heavily imbalanced output is `tongue_velocity` (90.9% \"not visible\"),\n   861→   which is a true property of the tongue and is handled by the harness' balanced loss and\n   862→   balanced-accuracy metric.\n   863→4. **Neural filtering checked** — quality filter + >1 Hz reproduce the paper's unit counts\n   864→   (Step 9); a control experiment (Step 8) showed that rescaling the firing rates does not\n   865→   improve accuracy.\n   866→5. **Processing matched to the reference code** — Step 10, Check 3.\n   867→\n   868→### Issues Found and Resolved\n   869→- *(from Step 10, carried here)* 64 trials with no electrophysiology → dropped.\n   870→- No reference)\n   893→\n   894→| Decision | Choice | Why |\n   895→|---|---|---|\n   896→| Sessions | 44 ALM ephys+video sessions from the reference load scripts (25 fixed + 19 randomized delay) | the only sessions with spikes; the load-script list reproduces the paper's n = 25 and n = 19 exactly. The two behaviour-only optogenetic directories have no neural data. |\n   897→| Alignment | go cue | required by the task; `params.alignEvent = 'goCue'` in all reference scripts |\n   898→| Window / bin | [−2.5, +2.5] s, 10 ms | `params.tmin/tmax/dt` in all reference scripts |\n   899→| Neural signal | causal-Gaussian-smoothed firing rate, spikes/s | `getSeq.m` + `mySmooth.m`; this is what the paper feeds to its own decoders. A control experiment showed rescaling does not help. |\n   900→| Unit curation | reference quality filter + >1 Hz | `findClusters.m`, `removeLowFRClusters.m`, Methods |\n   901→| Trial curation | drop early-lick, photostim and no-ephys trials; keep hit/miss/ignore and DR/WC | reference `params.condition`; ignore/none and WC/DR are decoder classes |\n   902→| Session curation | ≥10 units | Methods |\n   903→| Brain region | all units labelled `ALM` | the paper's recordings are in ALM; Data: Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.13941415 (copy in `/app/data`), code in `/app/code`, paper in `/app/paper.pdf`, methods in `/app/methods.txt`.\n- **Date started**: 2026-09-19\n- **Goal**: Convert to decoder-compatible format (`/app/converted_data.pkl`), go-cue aligned, for a neural decoder that predicts lick direction, behavioral context, outcome, tongue velocity, paw velocity and motion energy from ALM spiking activity.\n\n---\n\n## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nDirectory contents of `/app`:\n- `.manifest`, `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yaml` — container metadata\n- `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` — this file\n- `code/` — MATLAB code released with the paper\n- `data/` — four data subdirectories (see Step 2)\n- `decoder.py`, `train_decoder.py` — provided decoder / validation harness\n- `methods.txt`, `paper.pdf` — reference text\n\nEnvironment verified:\n- `python3` 3.13, `numpy` 2.4.4, `torch` 2.6.0+cu124, CUDA available (NVIDIA L4, 23 GB)\n- 128 CPUs, 1 TB RAM\n- Installed `pymatreader` (+`pypdf`) — needed because the `.mat` files are a mix of\n  MATLAB v7.3 (HDF5) and MATLAB v7 (MAT5) formats; `pymatreader` reads both with a\n  single uniform interface.\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `loadObjs` | `DataLoadingScripts/loadObjs.m` | LOADING | `load()` each `data_structure_<ANM>_<DATE>.mat` into struct array `obj` |\n| `load<ANM>_ALMVideo` | `DataLoadingScripts/Recording and video/*.m` | LOADING / CURATION | **Defines the analysed session list** and, per session, **which probe(s) are the ALM probes**. Commented-out entries are sessions the authors excluded. |\n| `loadSessionData` | `DataLoadingScripts/loadSessionData.m` | LOADING | Loops sessions × probes, calls `processData`, concatenates 2-probe sessions |\n| `processData` | `DataLoadingScripts/processData.m` | PROCESSING | Orchestrates `findTrials` → `findClusters` → `alignSpikes` → `getSeq` → `removeLowFRClusters` → `baselineFR` |\n| `findTrials` | `DataLoadingScripts/findTrials.m` | CURATION | Evaluates `params.condition` strings (e.g. `'R&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'`) against `obj.bp` fields to get trial indices per condition |\n| `findClusters` | `DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m` | CURATION | With `params.quality={'all'}` keeps every cluster whose (trimmed) quality label is **not** `garbage`, `gabrga`, `noisy`, `real?` (case-sensitive) |\n| `alignSpikes` | `DataLoadingScripts/alignSpikes.m` | ALIGNMENT | `clu.trialtm_aligned = clu.trialtm - ev.(alignEvent)(clu.trial)`; for us `alignEvent='goCue'` |\n| `getSeq` | `DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m` | PROCESSING | `edges = tmin:dt:tmax`; `obj.time = edges(1:end-1)+dt/2`; per trial `histc` spike counts → `/dt` → `mySmooth(...,params.smooth,params.bctype)` → `obj.trialdat` (time × units × trials), units = spikes/s |\n| `removeLowFRClusters` | `DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m` | CURATION | Drops units with mean PSTH firing rate ≤ `params.lowFR` (1 Hz in all figure scripts) |\n| `mySmooth` | `utils/mySmooth.m` | PROCESSING | Causal Gaussian filter: `gausswin(15)`, first `floor(15/2)=7` taps zeroed, normalised, `conv(...,'same')`; `'reflect'` boundary = prepend first 15 samples, then trim |\n| `findVideoOffset` | `funcs/findVideoOffset.m` | ALIGNMENT | `vidshift = mode(sglx.bitcode.bitstart)/sglx.fs − mode(bp.ev.bitStart)` (≈0.49 s, 0.99 s for JEB19). **tongue is not smoothed and NaNs are kept**; all other features `fillmissing('nearest')` |\n| `findVelocity` | `funcs/kinematics/findVelocity.m` | PROCESSING | `xvel = gradient(x)`, `yvel = gradient(y)`; non-tongue features get a `median(diff(pos))` baseline-drift subtraction and `fillmissing('nearest')`; **tongue NaN velocities are set to 0** |\n| `getKinematics` / `getKinematicsFromVideo` | `funcs/kinematics/*.m` | PROCESSING | Assembles (xdisp, ydisp, xvel, yvel) per feature per view + tongue angle/length + motion energy into `kin.dat` (time × trials × features) |\n| `getPrevChoice` | `funcs/getPrevChoice.m` | OUTPUT DEF | **Definition of choice/lick direction**: `choice = (R & hit) | (L & miss)` (1 = right), `NaN` on `no` (ignore) trials |\n| `getOutcome` | `funcs/getOutcome.m` | OUTPUT DEF | `outcome = bp.hit`, `NaN` on `no` (ignore) trials |\n| `UseInclusionCritera` / `RemoveUnwantedSessions` | `utils/*.m` | CURATION | Drop sessions with ≤40 right-correct or ≤40 left-correct DR trials (used for a subset of figures) |\n| `NeuralChoiceDecoding.m` / `NeuralContextDecoding.m` | `ChoiceContextDecoding/` | ANALYSIS | The paper's own decoders: input `obj.trialdat` (time × units × trials), min-max normalised to [-1,1], 75 ms bins, logistic regression per time bin |\n\n### Parameters used consistently across every figure script\n```\nparams.alignEvent = 'goCue';\nparams.lowFR      = 1;            % Hz\nparams.tmin       = -2.5;         % s relative to align event\nparams.tmax       =  2.5;         % s\nparams.dt         = 1/100;        % 10 ms bins\nparams.smooth     = 15;           % causal gaussian window (samples)\nparams.bctype     = 'reflect';\nparams.quality    = {'all'}; The above is therefore the reference processing.)\n\n### Notes\n- Electrophysiology: spikes are already sorted; units must be **quality-filtered**\n  (`findClusters`) and **firing-rate filtered** (`removeLowFRClusters`, >1 Hz). No dF/F\n  (this is ephys, not imaging).\n- The neural data the paper feeds to its own decoders is `obj.trialdat`: **smoothed,\n  binned single-trial firing rates in spikes/s**, go-cue aligned, 10 ms bins, [-2.5, 2.5] s.\n- Video is sampled at 400 Hz and must be shifted by `findVideoOffset` before being\n  aligned to the go cue; there is no additional neural/video lag (`advance_movement = 0`).\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n`/app/data` has four subdirectories:\n\n| Directory | Contents | Neural data? |\n|---|---|---|\n| `Ephys_Behavior` | 25 `data_structure_*.mat` + 25 `motionEnergy_*.mat` | **yes** — fixed-delay DR task (12 of these are also two-context DR+WC sessions) |\n| `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior` | 22 `data_structure_*.mat` + 20 `motionEnergy_*.mat` | **yes** — randomized-delay DR task |\n| `DelayInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior` | behaviour-only optogenetic sessions | no |\n| `GoCueInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior` | behaviour-only optogenetic sessions | no |\n\nThe two `*_Behavior` (inhibition) directories contain **no ephys** (README: \"sessions under\nthe `Video only` subdirectory contain behavior only\") and are therefore unusable for a\n*neural* decoder. **Excluded.**\n\n`data_structure_<ANM>_<DATE>.mat` contains one struct `obj`:\n\n| Field | Contents used here |\n|---|---|\n| `obj.bp` | Bpod trial data: `Ntrials`, `R`, `L`, `hit`, `miss`, `no`, `early`, `autowater`, `stim.enable`, and `obj.bp.ev` with `bitStart`, `sample`, `delay`, `goCue`, `reward`, `lickL{}`, `lickR{}` (all times in seconds **within trial**) |\n| `obj.clu` | 1×nProbes cell; each is a struct array over clusters with `tm`, `trialtm` (spike time within trial), `trial` (1-based trial index), `quality`, `channel`/`site`, `spkWavs` |\n| `obj.traj` | 2×1 cell: `{1}` side cam, `{2}` bottom cam. difference\nthat confirms both the session identification and the unit-curation rule.\n\n### Processing Details\n- **Temporal alignment**: go cue (`obj.bp.ev.goCue`), which in WC (autowater) trials is the\n  time the water drop is presented. Window [-2.5, +2.5] s.\n- **Temporal binning**: 10 ms bins; spike counts → rate (÷dt) → causal Gaussian smoothing\n  (`mySmooth`, window 15 samples = 150 ms, 'reflect' boundary).\n- **Video alignment**: `frameTimes − vidshift − goCue`, `vidshift` from `findVideoOffset`\n  (0.49 s for all sessions except JEB19 where it is 0.99 s). Linear interpolation onto the\n  10 ms neural time base. No neural/video lag.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n1. Only the ALM probe(s) designated in the reference `load<ANM>_ALMVideo.m` scripts.\n2. Drop clusters whose trimmed quality label is `garbage`, `gabrga`, `noisy` or `real?`\n   (`findClusters` with `quality={'all'}`; note the comparison is case-sensitive, so the single\n   `Noisy` label in JEB6 is kept — reproduced exactly).\n3. Drop clusters with mean firing rate ≤ 1 Hz over the analysis window (`lowFR = 1`).\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n1. Drop `early` (early-lick) trials — explicitly excluded from all analyses in the Methods and\n   by `~early` in every `params.condition`.\n2. Drop photoinactivation trials (`stim.enable == 1`) — `~stim.enable` in every\n   `params.condition`; these perturb both neural activity and behaviour.\n3. **Keep** `hit`, `miss` and `no` (ignore) trials, and keep both `autowater` (WC) and\n   non-autowater (DR) trials: the decoder task requires `ignore` as an outcome class,\n   `none` as a lick-direction class and `WC`/`DR` as context classes.\n\n**Session curation rule**: ≥10 units after neuron curation (Methods).\n\n### Decoders Trained (in the paper)\n| Decoded variable | Method | Accuracy |\n|---|---|---|\n| Choice (L vs R), from CD_choice projection | logistic regression, ROC | AUC 0.86 ± 0.11 (ED Fig. 2c) |\n| Choice (L vs R), from neural population | logistic regression per 75 ms bin, 4-fold CV | ≈0.5 (chance) before sample, rising through the delay to ≈0.9 after the go cue (Fig. 3b) |\n| Choice, from video kinematics | same | rises from chance after the sample tone (Fig. 3b) |\n| Context (DR vs WC), from neural population | same | well above chance (≈0.7) even during the ITI, ≈0.9 around the response epoch (Fig. 4b) |\n| Context, from video kinematics | same | similar timecourse to neural (Fig. 4b) |\n| CD_context from kinematics (regression) | ridge regression | R² = 0.49 ± 0.22 (n = 12 sessions) |\n\nThese are the quantitative expectations against which the converted-data decoder is compared\nin Steps 11–12. Note the paper's decoders are trained **per session** on **balanced** subsets\nof correct left/right trials, whereas the provided harness trains one shared decoder over all\nsessions on all trials; exact numbers are therefore not directly comparable, but the\nqualitative expectation (choice and context well above chance, highest after the go cue) is.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Analysis parameters | `getDefaultParams.m`: `dt=1/200`, `lowFR=0.5` vs every figure script: `dt=1/100`, `lowFR=1` | — | 1 Hz FR threshold; 10 ms consistent with Fig. 2/3 panels | Use the figure-script values (`dt=1/100`, `lowFR=1`); `getDefaultParams` is unused by the shipped analyses |\n| Bin width comment | `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` comment says \"use a 5 ms bin width\" but the code sets `params.dt = 1/100` | — | — | Comment is stale; use 10 ms |\n| # mice, fixed delay | 10 distinct animals in the load scripts | 10 animals over 25 files | \"25 sessions, nine mice\" | Paper appears to under-count by one (EKH1 is absent from `NullPotent/SessionMeta.csv`, which lists 24 of the 25 sessions and 9 mice). ‖(dx/dbin, dy/dbin)‖ per visible segment; class 2 where the tongue is not labelled or the video does not cover the bin; else 0/1 by the **session** median of visible speeds | `findPosition`, `findVelocity`, `findVideoOffset` | time-varying |\n| `obj.traj{2}.ts[:, :2, 'top_paw'/'bottom_paw']` | `output[4]` `paw_velocity` | as above; speed averaged over whichever paw(s) are visible; class 2 only where **neither** paw is visible | same | time-varying |\n| `motionEnergy_*.mat` `me.data{trial}` (or `obj.me`) | `output[5]` `motion_energy` | interp to `obj.time` using `frameTimes − vidshift − goCue`; class 2 where the video does not cover the bin; else 0/1 by the **session** median of covered bins | `loadMotionEnergy` | time-varying |\n| `obj.ex.anm` / filename | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | 14 mice | `load<ANM>_ALMVideo` | |\n| ALM probe from load script | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | all units labelled `ALM` | `load<ANM>_ALMVideo`, paper | |\n\n### Key Decisions\n\n1. **Which sessions.** All 44 sessions that contain ALM electrophysiology *and* video and are\n   listed in the reference `DataLoadingScripts/Recording and video/load*_ALMVideo.m` scripts:\n   25 fixed-delay + 19 randomized-delay. The two behaviour-only optogenetic directories have no\n   spikes and cannot be used by a neural decoder. The 3 `data_structure` files on disk that the\n   load scripts omit are excluded (this reproduces the paper's n = 19 randomized-delay sessions).\n   *Rationale*: the requested outputs (lick direction, outcome, kinematics, motion energy) are\n   defined in every session; `context` is degenerate (all DR) in the 32 sessions without WC\n   blocks but is still a correct label there, and the harness computes **balanced** accuracy\n   pooled over sessions, so keeping them adds data without corrupting the context label. The\n   12 two-context sessions supply the WC class.\n2. **Alignment: go cue** (`obj.bp.ev.goCue`), as required by the task and as used by every\n   reference analysis (`params.alignEvent = 'goCue'`). In WC trials this field holds the\n   water-drop time, which is the matched event for that context.\n3. **Window and binning: [-2.5, +2.5] s, 10 ms bins (500 bins/trial)** — exactly\n   `params.tmin/tmax/dt` from every reference figure script. Same length for all trials/sessions.\n4. **Neural representation: smoothed firing rate in spikes/s** (`obj.trialdat`), i.e. the same\n   quantity the paper feeds to its own choice/context decoders, including the causal 150 ms\n   Gaussian kernel with `'reflect'` boundary handling.\n5. **Neuron curation**: reference quality filter + >1 Hz mean firing rate (Methods).\n   Brain region: `ALM` for all units (paper; probe selection from the load scripts).\n6. **Trial curation**: drop `early` (Methods: \"omitted from all analyses\") and `stim.enable`\n   (photoinactivation) trials. Keep hit/miss/ignore and DR/WC — required by the output spec.\n7. **Session curation**: ≥10 curated units (Methods) and ≥2 trials (harness requirement).\n8. **Discretisation threshold**: per session, the median (50th percentile) over all *valid*\n   (visible / video-covered) timepoints of all kept trials in that session. Invalid timepoints\n   are class 2 and are excluded from the percentile, otherwise the \"not visible\" bins (≈90% of\n   the tongue trace) would drag the threshold to 0.\n9. **\"Not visible\" / \"no video\"** covers both DLC drop-out and bins outside the video's temporal\n   coverage. The video starts ≈0.03 s into the trial and ends 5–9 s in, so short-delay trials\n   genuinely have no video at the start of the window and some randomized-delay trials have\n   none at the end. Three trials in the whole dataset have unusable `frameTimes` (all NaN);\n   their kinematic/ME outputs are entirely class 2.\n10. **Paw**: mean speed of the visible subset of {`top_paw`, `bottom_paw`} (bottom camera only,\n    as in the Methods). Using a single paw would make \"not visible\" reach 88–96% in some\n    sessions because DLC loses one paw or the other; the union is 0–17%.\n11. **Tongue**: side-camera `tongue` feature (`params.traj_features{1}{1}`), the canonical\n    tongue-tip marker.\n12. **Velocity units** are pixels per 10 ms bin (the reference's `gradient()` on the interpolated\n    position, no unit conversion). Only the within-session rank matters after the median split.\n13. **No neural/video lag** (`params.advance_movement = 0`), per all reference scripts.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Session/mouse counts: 44 sessions, 14 mice; fixed-delay subset 25/10; randomized 19/4;\n      two-context subset 12 sessions.\n- [ ] Unit counts vs. paper: two-context subset ≈522 pre-FR-filter (we get 528).\n- [ ] Every session ≥10 units; report the distribution (paper ED Fig. 2a).\n- [ ] `hit + miss + no == Ntrials` per session; kept trials = Ntrials − |early ∪ stim|.\n- [ ] Input range exactly [-2.495, 2.495]; identical time base for every trial.\n- [ ] Output 0/1/2 fractions per variable; tongue class-2 fraction ≈0.9 (matches raw DLC NaN rate).\n- [ ] Per session, class-0 and class-1 counts for each discretised output are equal ±1\n      (median split by construction).\n- [ ] Spike-count spot check: recompute the binned rate for a specific (session, trial, neuron)\n      straight from the raw `.mat` and compare with `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] Behaviour spot check: recompute lick direction / outcome / context for specific trials\n      from `bp` fields.\n- [ ] Kinematics spot check: recompute the interpolated tongue/paw position and motion energy\n      for a specific trial and verify the class assignment.\n- [ ] Alignment check: mean firing rate and mean motion energy locked to the go cue should show\n      a sharp rise at t = 0 (plots in `--show-processing`).\n\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` implements the plan above. Structure:\n\n| Function | Mirrors |\n|---|---|\n| `load_obj` | `loadObjs.m` (via `pymatreader`, handles MAT v7 and v7.3) |\n| `select_clusters` | `findClusters.m` with `quality={'all'}` |\n| `select_trials` | the `~stim.enable & ~early` part of every `params.condition` |\n| `causal_gaussian` / `my_smooth` | `mySmooth.m` (`gausswin(15)`, first 7 taps zeroed, `'reflect'`) |\n| `bin_index` | `histc(t, tmin:dt:tmax)` then `N(1:end-1)` |\n| `bin_and_smooth` | `alignSpikes.m` + `getSeq.m` → `obj.trialdat` |\n| `video_shift` | `findVideoOffset.m` |\n| `interp_feature` | `findPosition.m` |\n| `feature_speed` | `findVelocity.m` |\n| `load_motion_energy` + `interp_trace` | `loadMotionEnergy.m` |\n| mean-rate filter in `process_session` | `removeLowFRClusters.m` |\n\nCLI: `python -u /app/convert_data.py <out.pkl> [--full|--sample] [--show-processing] [--workers N]`.\n\n**Verification of the two most error-prone primitives** (`/app/cache/test_primitives.py`):\n- `my_smooth` reproduces a literal transcription of `mySmooth.m`\n  (`np.convolve(concat(x[:15], x), kern, 'same')[15:]`) to 2.2e-16, and an impulse at\n  bin 100 produces no output before bin 100 (causality) with its peak exactly at bin 100.\n- `causal_gaussian()` = `[0]*7 + [0.25098, 0.23547, 0.19447, 0.14137, 0.09046, 0.05096,\n  0.02527, 0.01103]`, sums to 1 — matches `gausswin(15)` (alpha = 2.5) with `kern(1:7)=0`.\n- `bin_index` reproduces `histc` edge semantics: `t = -2.5 → bin 0`, `t = 0 → bin 250`\n  (whose centre is +0.005 s), `t = 2.4999 → bin 499`, `t = 2.5 → dropped` (this is the\n  `N(1:end-1)` step), `t` outside `[-2.5, 2.5)` dropped.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- `pymatreader` reads the whole `obj`, including `clu.spkWavs`, which dominates the runtime\n  (6–8 s and ~1.4 GB for the largest 290 MB session).\n- Naive per-trial × per-neuron Python loops for binning would be ~10^4 iterations/session.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Spike binning is a single `np.bincount` per cluster over a flattened `(trial, bin)` index\n  instead of a per-trial loop.\n- Smoothing is applied once per session to a `(515, nclusters·ntrials)` matrix, as an 8-tap\n  causal FIR (the kernel's first 7 taps are zero), so no FFT and no round-off.\n- Sessions are processed in parallel with `ProcessPoolExecutor` (12–16 workers; each worker\n  peaks at ~1.5 GB, well within the 1 TB available).\n- Outputs are stored as `int8` and the (identical) time-ramp input array is shared between\n  trials, which keeps the pickle dominated by the neural `float32` arrays.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: All were inspected and show no anomalies:\n1. **raster → binning → smoothing** for the highest-rate unit of one trial: the smoothed\n   trace rises only *after* each spike (causal kernel) and its peak height matches the\n   binned rate scaled by the kernel peak.\n2. **Go-cue-aligned population PSTH**: a sharp rise exactly at t = 0 and, for the\n   fixed-delay session, a second bump at t ≈ −2.2 s — the sample-tone onset\n   (`ev.sample` = 0.3 s, `ev.goCue` = 2.5 s → −2.2 s). Licks occur after the go cue, as they must.\n5. **Motion energy**: raw and interpolated traces coincide; the class trace switches at the\n   plotted session median.\n6. **Class fractions** bar chart (see table above).\n7. **Per-trial outputs over the session**: the `context` trace shows the ~100 initial DR\n   trials followed by alternating DR/WC blocks — exactly the design described in the Methods.\n8. **P(motion energy above median) vs time, split by context**: a sharp movement onset at\n   the go cue in both contexts, with the DR context ramping up during the delay\n   (the paper's \"uninstructed movements during the delay epoch\").\n\n### Run Time Estimates\n\n| Speed-ups Implemented | Time Savings |\n|---|---|\n| `np.bincount` binning instead of per-trial `histogram` | ~20× on the binning step |\n| single session-wide FIR smoothing instead of per-trial `conv` | ~50× on the smoothing step |\n| 12-way process parallelism | ~8× wall clock |\n\n| Step | Time / Session | Estimated Total (44 sessions) |\n|---|---|---|\n| `.mat` load (`pymatreader`) | 4.2–7.6 s | 250 s serial |\n| spike binning + smoothing | 0.2–0.4 s | 15 s |\n| video/kinematics/ME | 0.2–0.4 s | 15 s |\n| **total, 12 workers** | — | **~45 s** wall + ~30 s to pickle ≈ **1.5 min** |\n\nWell under the 15-minute budget, so no further optimisation was needed. (Sessions differ in\nsize; the estimate above uses the largest observed per-session load time, 7.6 s, for the\n25 fixed-delay sessions, which are the big ones.)\n\n### Format verification\n`/app/verification_sample_out.txt`: **\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\"**\nAll per-session `T` = 500, input range [-2.5, 2.5], output ranges 0–2 / 0–1 as designed,\n`ALM: 98 neurons`.\n\n---\n\n## Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl` → `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n\n### Format Validation\n- Errors: None\n- Warnings: None\n\n### Training\nLoss decreased monotonically, 8.10 → 0.734 over 200 epochs; test loss 0.791.\n\n### Decoder Results (Sample)\n| Output | Chance | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc |\n|--------|--------|-------------|--------|\n| lick_direction | 0.333 | 0.629 | 0.591 |\n| context | 0.500 | 0.808 | 0.814 |\n| outcome | 0.333 | 0.628 | 0.557 |\n| tongue_velocity | 0.333 | 0.585 | 0.573 |\n| paw_velocity | 0.333 | 0.647 | 0.634 |\n| motion_energy | 0.333 | 0.759 | 0.751 |\n\nEvery output is above chance; train and validation are close (no overfitting).\nContext 0.81 on a genuine two-context session is in line with the paper's neural context\ndecoding (Fig. 4b).\n\n### Neural-scaling control experiment\nBecause the paper's own decoder min-max normalises the neural matrix to [-1, 1]\n(`NeuralChoiceDecoding.m`), two alternative neural representations were tested on the\nsample before committing to raw rates:\n\n| Neural representation | lick | context | outcome | tongue | paw | ME |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **firing rate, spikes/s (chosen)** | 0.61 | 0.76 | 0.61 | 0.65 | 0.54 | 0.77 |\n| min-max to [-1, 1] (per session) | 0.57 | 0.72 | 0.57 | 0.61 | 0.51 | 0.72 |\n| per-neuron z-score (soft) | 0.62 | 0.75 | 0.59 | 0.66 | 0.55 | 0.76 |\n\n(run on the first sample pair, JEB13 2022-09-13 + JEB11 2022-05-10). Investigating the raw\nfiles showed that in those two sessions the **SpikeGLX recording stops before the\nbehavioural session does**: the largest trial index appearing in any cluster's spike train\nis 314 of 343, and 312 of 346 respectively. JEB12 2022-05-12 similarly has one trial\n(index 192) with no spikes, which `obj.trials.bp.haveEphys` also flags as 0.\n`trials_with_ephys()` was added to `convert_data.py`: a trial is dropped when **no**\ncluster on the ALM probe(s) — including clusters the quality filter rejects — has a single\nspike on it. That removes 64 trials (29 + 34 + 1) and eliminates all warnings.\n(`obj.trials.bp.haveEphys` was not used directly because its length disagrees with\n`bp.Ntrials` in the one session where it is informative — 319 entries for 318 trials.)\n\n### Consistency Check\n| Statistic | Reference Paper | Reference Code | Reference Data | Converted Data | Match? It imports **only the session table**\nfrom `convert_data.py`, never the processing functions. Provenance for the comparison\ncomes from the `trial_index` and `cluster_index` arrays now stored in\n`metadata['session_info']`.\n\nChecks run on 4 sessions (`JEB6_2021-04-18`, `JEB13_2022-09-14`, `JEB23_2023-10-11`,\n`JEB24_2023-11-03`), 2 trials and 2 neurons each — **all 44 assertions pass**:\n\n| Check | Example result |\n|---|---|\n| neural firing-rate trace, (session, trial, neuron) | `max|diff| = 3.7e-06` (float32 storage precision), e.g. 108 spikes → peak 98.0 spk/s |\n| lick_direction from `bp.R/L/hit/miss/no` on 3 trials/session | e.g. `R=1,L=0,hit=1 → right`; `R=0,L=1,no=1 → none` |\n| context from `bp.autowater` | exact |\n| outcome from `bp.hit/no` | exact |\n| tongue / paw / motion-energy class vectors for a whole trial | exact (`np.array_equal`) |\n| stored per-session threshold really is the median of valid timepoints | class-1 count ≥ class-0 count, imbalance < 5% |\n| `input` == bin centres, identical for every trial of every session | `[-2.495 .. 2.495]` |\n| trial bookkeeping (`ntrials_kept == len(trial_index)`, unique, in range) | all 44 sessions |\n\nOutput: `ALL SANITY CHECKS PASSED`.\n\nAdditional primitive tests: `/app/cache/test_primitives.py` (Step 6) — kernel, smoother\nand binning semantics.\n\n### Check 3 — Reference code comparison\n\n| Stage | Reference (MATLAB) | This conversion | Same? |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **(a) data loading** | `loadObjs.m`: `load(meta(i).datapth)`; session list and ALM probe from `load<ANM>_ALMVideo.m`; motion energy from `motionEnergy_<ANM>_<DATE>.mat` (`loadMotionEnergy.m`) | `load_obj` (pymatreader), identical session/probe table hard-coded from the same scripts, `load_motion_energy` reads the same files (falling back to `obj.me` for the JEB23/JEB24 layout the reference loader does not handle) | ✅ |\n| **(b) neuron filtering** | `findClusters(quality,'all')`: drop `garbage`/`gabrga`/`noisy`/`real?` (case-sensitive, `strtrim`ed); then `removeLowFRClusters` with `params.lowFR = 1` | `select_clusters` — identical string set and case sensitivity; then mean rate over the analysis window > 1 Hz | ✅ |\n| **(b) trial filtering** | every `params.condition` contains `~stim.enable&~early`; conditions also split by `hit/miss/no`, `R/L`, `autowater`, but only to *label* trials | `select_trials` drops `early` and `stim.enable`; hit/miss/no and DR/WC are kept and used as labels, as the decoder-output spec demands | ✅ (plus the no-ephys rule, see below) |\n| **(c) temporal alignment** | `alignSpikes.m`: `trialtm − bp.ev.goCue(trial)`. Video: `frameTimes − findVideoOffset(obj) − ev.goCue(trial)` (`findPosition.m`, `loadMotionEnergy.m`), `params.advance_movement = 0` | identical formulas, same `findVideoOffset` | ✅ |\n| **(d) binning** | `getSeq.m`: `edges = -2.5:0.01:2.5`, `histc`, drop the last bin, `/dt`, `mySmooth(...,15,'reflect')`; `obj.time = edges(1:end-1) + dt/2` | `bin_index` + `np.bincount` (exact `histc` semantics, verified), `/DT`, `my_smooth` (verified equal to `mySmooth` to 2e-16) | ✅ |\n| **(e) input construction** | `obj.time` is the reference time base | `input[0] = obj.time` broadcast over the trial | ✅ (the input set is dictated by the task spec) |\n| **(f) output construction** | `getPrevChoice.m` `choice = (R&hit)|(L&miss)`, NaN on `no`; `getOutcome.m` `outcome = hit`, NaN on `no`; context from `autowater`; kinematics from `findPosition`/`findVelocity`; motion energy from `loadMotionEnergy` | identical definitions, with the reference's `NaN` on ignore trials replaced by the explicit third class the task spec requires (`none` / `ignore`) | ✅ with the spec-mandated change |\n\n**Deliberate differences, and why**\n\n1. **Trials with no electrophysiology are dropped** (64 trials). The reference has no such\n   rule because the MATLAB pipeline simply leaves those trials as all-zero rows in\n   `obj.trialdat` and the figures use trial subsets that happen to avoid them. An all-zero\n   neural matrix is not data and the harness flags it; dropping is required for a decoder.\n2. **`ignore` trials keep a label instead of `NaN`.** `getOutcome.m`/`getPrevChoice.m` set\n   NaN so that MATLAB analyses skip them. The task spec requires `none` and `ignore` as\n   explicit classes, so they are coded 2.\n3. **Velocity is differentiated per contiguous visible segment.** `findVelocity.m` runs\n   `gradient()` over the whole trial and then replaces NaN with 0 (tongue) or the nearest\n   value (other features), which is appropriate for assembling a dense regressor matrix but\n   injects spurious zero velocities at the edge of every visibility gap. Because the task\n   spec makes \"not visible\" its own class, the velocity only has to be defined where the\n   feature *is* visible, and a per-segment derivative is the correct estimate there. The\n   reference's non-tongue baseline-drift subtraction (`median(diff(pos))`) is reproduced,\n   including its use of `basederiv(1)` for both axes (an apparent typo whose numerical\n   effect is negligible — both medians are ≈0 px/bin).\n4. **Paw velocity is the mean over the visible paws** rather than one fixed marker: DLC\n   loses `top_paw` on up to 88% of frames in some sessions and `bottom_paw` on up to 96% in\n   others, so a single marker would make \"not visible\" the dominant class for reasons that\n   have nothing to do with the animal. The union is unavailable on only 0.6–24% of bins.\n5. **No PCA/dimensionality reduction of the kinematic features** (`getKinematics.m` does\n   this for its regression analyses). The decoder task asks for specific discretised\n   kinematic variables, not latent factors.\n6. **Both fixed-delay and randomized-delay sessions are included in one dataset.** The paper\n   analyses them separately; the harness trains a per-session projection, so mixing them is\n   harmless and keeps all available ALM recordings with video.\n\n### Check 4 — Key statistics comparison\nSee the table in Step 9. How are **all the data** for all subjects, sessions, and trials loaded in?\n\ni. Each session is loaded from a `data_structure_<ANM>_<DATE>.mat` file using `pymatreader.read_mat()`, which handles both MATLAB v7 and v7.3 formats transparently. Motion energy is loaded separately from `motionEnergy_<ANM>_<DATE>.mat` files using the same library. The 44 sessions and their ALM probe numbers are hard-coded in `SESSIONS` (as `FIXED_DELAY` + `RANDOMIZED_DELAY` lists), transcribed from the authors' `load<ANM>_ALMVideo.m` scripts. Data directories are split by task type (`Ephys_Behavior` and `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior`). Sessions are processed in parallel using `ProcessPoolExecutor`.\n\nii.\n```python\nFIXED_DELAY = [\n    (\"JEB6\", \"2021-04-18\", [2]),\n    ...\n]\nRANDOMIZED_DELAY = (\n    [(\"JEB11\", \"2022-05-10\", [1]), ...]\n    + [(\"JEB23\", d, [1]) for d in (...)]\n    + [(\"JEB24\", d, [1]) for d in (...)]\n)\nSESSIONS = ([(a, d, p, \"fixed\") for a, d, p in FIXED_DELAY]\n            + [(a, d, p, \"randomized\") for a, d, p in RANDOMIZED_DELAY])\n\ndef load_obj(anm, date, task):\n    from pymatreader import read_mat\n    return read_mat(data_path(anm, date, task))[\"obj\"]\n```\n\niii. The session list is taken verbatim from the authors' `DataLoadingScripts/Recording and video/load<ANM>_ALMVideo.m` scripts, excluding sessions the authors commented out and 3 extra files on disk not in the load scripts. This reproduces the paper's n=25 fixed-delay and n=19 randomized-delay sessions exactly. `pymatreader` was chosen because it reads both MAT v7 and v7.3 with a single interface.\n\n## 1-b. How are the data split into subjects?\n\ni. The animal name is the first element of each session tuple (e.g., `\"JEB19\"`). At assembly, `subjects` is the sorted set of unique animal names, and `subject_idx` maps each session to its index in that list. The 44 sessions come from 14 animals.\n\nii.\n```python\nsubjects = sorted({r[\"info\"][\"anm\"] for _, r in kept})\ndata = {\n    ...\n    \"subjects\": subjects,\n    \"subject_idx\": np.array([subjects.index(r[\"info\"][\"anm\"]) for _, r in kept],\n                            dtype=np.int64),\n    ...\n}\n```\n\niii. The animal name is stored directly in the session tuple, which comes from the load scripts. This is consistent with how the authors identify animals.\n\n## 1-c. How are the data split into sessions?\n\ni. One session = one entry in the `SESSIONS` list = one `.mat` file on disk. Each session is identified by `(anm, date, probes, task)`. The task type determines which data directory to look in. Each session becomes one element in the `neural`, `input`, and `output` lists. The result is 44 sessions: 25 fixed-delay and 19 randomized-delay.\n\nii.\n```python\nDATA_DIR = {\"fixed\": \"/app/data/Ephys_Behavior\",\n            \"randomized\": \"/app/data/RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior\"}\n\ndef data_path(anm, date, task):\n    return os.path.join(DATA_DIR[task], f\"data_structure_{anm}_{date}.mat\")\n```\n\niii. Sessions match the authors' load scripts exactly.\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trials are defined by the Bpod structure (`obj.bp`), with `Ntrials` giving the count. Each per-trial field (hit, miss, R, L, etc.) has one entry per trial. Spike times carry a `trial` field (1-based) indicating which trial each spike belongs to. No trial boundaries need to be reconstructed.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef select_trials(bp, obj=None, probes=()):\n    n = int(vec(bp[\"Ntrials\"])[0])\n    early = vec(bp[\"early\"])[:n] > 0\n    stim = vec(bp[\"stim\"][\"enable\"])[:n] > 0\n    keep = ~(early | stim)\n    if obj is not None:\n        keep &= trials_with_ephys(obj, probes, n)\n    return np.flatnonzero(keep), n\n```\n\niii. The Bpod table directly defines trials with one go cue per trial, so no inference is needed.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Three filters are applied: (1) Early-lick trials (`bp.early`) are dropped, following the paper's Methods (\"omitted from analyses\"). (2) Photostimulation trials (`bp.stim.enable`) are dropped, as in every `params.condition` in the reference code. (3) Trials with no electrophysiology data are dropped via `trials_with_ephys()`, which detects trials where no cluster on the ALM probe has any spike (64 trials across the dataset, concentrated in two JEB24 sessions where the SpikeGLX recording stopped early). This keeps 13,762 of 14,972 raw trials.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef trials_with_ephys(obj, probes, ntrials):\n    ...\n    has = np.zeros(ntrials, dtype=bool)\n    for p in probes:\n        c = clu[p - 1]\n        ...\n        for t in trialno:\n            ...\n            has[a] = True\n    return has\n\ndef select_trials(bp, obj=None, probes=()):\n    n = int(vec(bp[\"Ntrials\"])[0])\n    early = vec(bp[\"early\"])[:n] > 0\n    stim = vec(bp[\"stim\"][\"enable\"])[:n] > 0\n    keep = ~(early | stim)\n    if obj is not None:\n        keep &= trials_with_ephys(obj, probes, n)\n    return np.flatnonzero(keep), n\n```\n\niii. Early-lick and photostim removal follows the paper's Methods and every `params.condition`. The no-ephys filter is needed because some trials have no neural data (recording ended), which would create all-zero neural matrices. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES document this was added after discovering 61 warnings in the first full conversion run.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. `obj.clu{probe}` provides the spike-sorted clusters. Each cluster has `trial` (1-based trial index), `trialtm` (spike time relative to trial start), and `quality` (manual curation label). `bp.ev.goCue` provides the alignment times.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrialtm = c[\"trialtm\"] if isinstance(c[\"trialtm\"], list) else [c[\"trialtm\"]]\ntrialno = c[\"trial\"] if isinstance(c[\"trial\"], list) else [c[\"trial\"]]\ntm = np.ravel(np.asarray(trialtm[i], dtype=np.float64))\ntr = np.ravel(np.asarray(trialno[i], dtype=np.float64)).astype(np.int64) - 1\n...\naligned = tm - align_times[tr]   # alignSpikes.m\n```\n\niii. These are the same variables used by `alignSpikes.m` and `getSeq.m` in the reference code.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spikes are aligned to the go cue (`trialtm - goCue`), binned into 10 ms bins over [-2.5, 2.5] s (500 bins), converted to firing rates (counts / dt), and smoothed with a causal Gaussian kernel that exactly reproduces the reference `mySmooth.m`: `gausswin(15)` with the first 7 taps zeroed and a `'reflect'` boundary condition. The result is firing rates in spikes/s, matching `obj.trialdat` from the MATLAB pipeline. Units from both probes of a two-probe session are concatenated.\n\nii.\n```python\nTMIN, TMAX, DT = -2.5, 2.5, 1.0 / 100.0   # params.tmin / params.tmax / params.dt\nSMOOTH_N = 15\n\ndef causal_gaussian(n=SMOOTH_N, alpha=2.5):\n    k = np.arange(n)\n    w = np.exp(-0.5 * (alpha * (k - (n - 1) / 2.0) / ((n - 1) / 2.0)) ** 2)\n    w[: n // 2] = 0.0\n    return w / w.sum()\n\ndef bin_and_smooth(obj, cluster_ids, align_times, ntrials):\n    ...\n    aligned = tm - align_times[tr]\n    b = bin_index(aligned)\n    ...\n    flat = tr[ok] * NT + b[ok]\n    counts[ci] = np.bincount(flat, minlength=ntrials * NT).reshape(ntrials, NT)\n    rate = counts / DT\n    m = rate.reshape(-1, NT).T\n    m = my_smooth(m)\n    return m.T.reshape(len(cluster_ids), ntrials, NT)\n```\n\niii. The AI explicitly verified that `my_smooth` reproduces `mySmooth.m` to machine precision (2.2e-16) and that `causal_gaussian` matches `gausswin(15)` with the first 7 taps zeroed. The 10 ms bin size and causal Gaussian smoothing match `params.dt = 1/100` and `params.smooth = 15` as used in every figure script. The AI noted that `getDefaultParams.m` has `dt=1/200` but every actual analysis script overrides to `1/100`.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters: (1) Quality filter (`select_clusters`): clusters with quality label matching `garbage`, `gabrga`, `noisy`, or `real?` are dropped, with case-sensitive, trimmed comparison exactly as in `findClusters.m`. (2) Firing rate filter: units with mean rate <= 1 Hz over the analysis window are dropped (`removeLowFRClusters`). Additionally, sessions with < 10 curated units are dropped (Methods).\n\nii.\n```python\nQUALITY_REJECT = (\"garbage\", \"gabrga\", \"noisy\", \"real?\")\nLOW_FR = 1.0\nMIN_UNITS = 10\n\ndef select_clusters(obj, probes):\n    ...\n    for i, q in enumerate(quals):\n        q = str(q).strip() if isinstance(q, (str, np.str_)) else \"\"\n        if q not in QUALITY_REJECT:\n            keep.append((p - 1, i))\n    return keep\n\n# In process_session:\n    mean_fr = rates.reshape(n_quality, -1).mean(axis=1)\n    keep_unit = mean_fr > LOW_FR\n    rates = rates[keep_unit]\n```\n\niii. The quality drop set exactly matches `findClusters.m` with `quality={'all'}`. The comparison is case-sensitive as in MATLAB's `ismember`. The 1 Hz threshold matches `params.lowFR = 1` used in all figure scripts and the paper's Methods. The >=10 units session filter comes from the Methods.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Spike times are aligned to the go cue by subtracting `bp.ev.goCue[trial]` from each spike's `trialtm`. This is the same as `alignSpikes.m`: `clu.trialtm_aligned = clu.trialtm - ev.(alignEvent)(clu.trial)`.\n\nii.\n```python\naligned = tm - align_times[tr]   # alignSpikes.m\n```\n\niii. This exactly reproduces `alignSpikes.m` with `params.alignEvent = 'goCue'`.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. 10 ms bins, 500 bins spanning [-2.5, 2.5] s from the go cue. The bin edges are `np.arange(-2.5, 2.5 + dt/2, dt)` (501 edges, 500 bins) and the bin centres are the midpoints. No temporal rebinning is applied — spikes are counted directly into these bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nTMIN, TMAX, DT = -2.5, 2.5, 1.0 / 100.0\nEDGES = np.round(np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT / 2.0, DT), 10)   # 501 bin edges\nTIME = (EDGES[:-1] + DT / 2.0).astype(np.float64)            # 500 bin centres\nNT = TIME.size\n```\n\niii. The AI explicitly checked that `getDefaultParams.m` has `dt=1/200` (5ms) but every actual analysis script (`Scripts/Figure*`, `Scripts/EDFigure*`, etc.) overrides this to `params.dt = 1/100` (10ms). The AI chose 10ms as the actual processing used by the paper. The window [-2.5, 2.5] matches `params.tmin`/`params.tmax`.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. Not derived from raw data variables. It is computed as the bin centres of the analysis window: evenly spaced values from -2.495 to +2.495 s in 10 ms steps.\n\nii.\n```python\nTIME = (EDGES[:-1] + DT / 2.0).astype(np.float64)  # 500 bin centres\ninput_trial = np.ascontiguousarray(TIME[None, :], dtype=np.float32)\n```\n\niii. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction* derived from?\n\ni. Four per-trial fields: `bp.R` (instructed right), `bp.L` (instructed left), `bp.hit` (correct response), and `bp.miss` (incorrect response). The lick direction is not directly recorded but can be inferred from the instructed side and outcome, following `getPrevChoice.m`.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction*?\n\ni. Left lick = `(L & hit) | (R & miss)` (code 0), Right lick = `(R & hit) | (L & miss)` (code 1), None = `no` (ignore trials, code 2). This follows the formula in `getPrevChoice.m`.\n\nii.\n```python\nlick = np.full(ntrials_all, 2, dtype=np.int8)          # 2 = none\nlick[(L & hit) | (R & miss)] = 0                        # left\nlick[(R & hit) | (L & miss)] = 1                        # right\nlick[no] = 2                                            # getPrevChoice: NaN on ignore\n```\n\niii. A hit means the animal licked the correct (instructed) side, a miss means it licked the wrong side. This is the same logic as `getPrevChoice.m` where `choice = (R & hit) | (L & miss)`.\n\n## 5-a.
